<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Every compactly supported integral <i>n</i>-cycle, <i>n</i> ≥ 2, in a proper CAT(0) space bounds a compactly supported integral current with the sharp Euclidean mass bound. The theorem allows arbitrary integer multiplicities and unrestricted ambient dimension. In particular, we prove the Euclidean Cartan–Hadamard isoperimetric conjecture in dimensions at least three.

- **claim-sharp-integral-filling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $X$ be a proper CAT(0) space and $n\ge2$ an integer. Every compactly supported $T\in\mathbf I_n(X)$ with $\partial T=0$ admits a compactly supported $S\in\mathbf I_{n+1}(X)$ such that $$\label{eq:main} \partial S=T,\qquad \mathbf M(S)\le c_n\mathbf M(T)^{(n+1)/n} =\frac{\mathbf M(T)^{(n+1)/n}} {(n+1)^{(n+1)/n}\omega_{n+1}^{1/n}}.$$ There is no restriction on the dimension of $X$ or on the integer multiplicities of the currents. The coefficient is optimal.
  - Explanation: Every eligible integral cycle admits a compactly supported integral filling with the optimal Euclidean mass coefficient. Ambient dimension and integer multiplicities are unrestricted.
- **claim-cartan-hadamard-domain-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-cartan-hadamard)): Let $M$ be a Cartan–Hadamard manifold of dimension $d\ge3$. For every bounded domain $\Omega\subset M$ with smooth boundary, $$\label{eq:classical} \operatorname{area}(\partial\Omega) \ge d\omega_d^{1/d}\operatorname{vol}(\Omega)^{(d-1)/d}.$$ The same inequality holds for relatively compact sets of finite perimeter, with ambient perimeter in place of boundary area.
  - Explanation: Boundary currents yield the Euclidean volume–perimeter inequality for bounded smooth domains and relatively compact sets of finite perimeter in Cartan–Hadamard manifolds.
- **claim-compact-ambient-reduction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-compact-reduction)): Suppose that the asserted filling inequality in a fixed cycle dimension holds in every compact $\operatorname{CAT}(0)$ space. Then it holds for every compactly supported integral cycle of that dimension in every proper $\operatorname{CAT}(0)$ space, with a compactly supported integral filling.
  - Explanation: The compact-space filling inequality transfers to compactly supported cycles in proper CAT(0) spaces through an enclosing compact convex ball.
- **claim-compact-current-background** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-current-background)): Let $Y$ be compact and $\operatorname{CAT}(0)$, and let $k\ge1$. The following statements hold.* 1. *Lipschitz pushforward preserves integral currents, commutes with boundary, and satisfies the Lipschitz mass bound. Restrictions by bounded Borel coefficients have the usual mass-measure bound; for a Borel set $E\subset Y$, $$\\|C\mathbin{\vrule height 1.4ex depth -0.3ex width .07ex\vrule height .07ex depth 0ex width .7ex}E\\|=\\|C\\|\mathbin{\vrule height 1.4ex depth -0.3ex width .07ex\vrule height .07ex depth 0ex width .7ex}E.$$ The restricted current need not be normal. If $u:Y\to\mathbb R$ is Lipschitz and $C\in\mathbf I_k(Y)$, then $C\mathbin{\vrule height 1.4ex depth -0.3ex width .07ex\vrule height .07ex depth 0ex width .7ex}\{u>t\}$ is integral for almost every $t$. In particular its boundary is an integral $(k-1)$-cycle.* 2. *If $C_j\in\mathbf I_k(Y)$ and $\sup_j(\mathbf M(C_j)+\mathbf M(\partial C_j))<\infty$, a subsequence converges weakly to an integral current. Boundary commutes with weak convergence, and mass is lower semicontinuous.* 3. *Every $C\in\mathbf I_k(Y)$ admits a representation by bi-Lipschitz maps $\phi_i:E_i\to Y$, where $E_i\subset\mathbb R^k$ are bounded Borel sets and the images $Z_i=\phi_i(E_i)$ are pairwise disjoint Borel sets, and by signed integer multiplicities $\theta_i\in L^1(E_i)$, such that $$\label{eq:chart-action} C(b,\pi_1,\ldots,\pi_k) =\sum_i\int_{E_i}\theta_i(z)b(\phi_i(z)) \det D\bigl((\pi_1,\ldots,\pi_k)\circ\phi_i\bigr)(z)\,dz.$$ The chart summands have summable masses. Zero multiplicities may be discarded. Scalar functions on a Borel chart domain are differentiated by Lipschitz extension to $\mathbb R^k$; their derivatives are independent of that extension at almost every density point of the domain.* 4. *For $h>0$, the whole-current product $[0,h]\times C$, with the Euclidean product metric, is an integral $(k+1)$-current and obeys $$\label{eq:interval-product-boundary} \partial([0,h]\times C) =[h]\times C-[0]\times C-[0,h]\times\partial C.$$ Writing $b_t(x)=b(t,x)$ and $\pi_{a,t}(x)=\pi_a(t,x)$, its action is $$\label{eq:interval-product-action} \begin{split} &([0,h]\times C)(b,\pi_1,\ldots,\pi_{k+1})\\ &\quad=\sum_{a=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{a+1}\int_0^h C\bigl(b_t\,\partial_t\pi_a(t,\cdot), \pi_{1,t},\ldots,\widehat{\pi_{a,t}},\ldots, \pi_{k+1,t}\bigr)\,dt. \end{split}$$ The hat indicates omission. The time derivative is interpreted almost everywhere and as a bounded Borel first coefficient in the current action.* 5. *There are finite constants $K_k,L_k$, independent of the cycle, such that every integral $k$-cycle $B$ in $Y$ satisfies $$\label{eq:coarse-fillings} V(B)\le K_k\mathbf M(B)^{(k+1)/k},\qquad V(B)\le L_k\mathbf M(B).$$ Every such $B$ has a mass-minimizing integral filling in $Y$. If integral $k$-cycles $B_j$ have bounded masses and converge weakly to an integral cycle $B$, then $$\label{eq:filling-continuity} V(B_j-B)\mathrel{\begingroup \let\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\endcsname \let\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\endcsname \let\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\endcsname \let\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname \let\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\endcsname \let\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\endcsname \pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}% PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\endgroup}0, \qquad V(B_j)\mathrel{\begingroup \let\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\endcsname \let\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\endcsname \let\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\endcsname \let\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname \let\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\endcsname \let\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\endcsname \pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}% PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\endgroup}V(B).$$
  - Explanation: Collects current calculus, rectifiable representation, weak compactness, whole-current products, coarse filling bounds, attainment, and filling continuity in the compact ambient space.
- **claim-positive-variational-extremizer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-extremizer)): Let $c>0$. If the functional $$B\mathrel{\begingroup \let\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\endcsname \let\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\endcsname \let\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\endcsname \let\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname \let\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\endcsname \let\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\endcsname \pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27FC> >> BDC}% PPaperOriginallongmapsto\endcsname\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\endgroup}V(B)-c\mathbf M(B)^{q_0}$$ is positive on some integral $n$-cycle in $Y$, it attains a positive maximum on such cycles. A maximizing cycle $A$, with $m=\mathbf M(A)>0$, satisfies $$\label{eq:extremizer} c\bigl(m^{q_0}-\mathbf M(B)^{q_0}\bigr)\le V(A-B) \qquad\text{for every integral \(n\)-cycle }B\text{ in }Y.$$
  - Explanation: A positive violation of a penalized filling functional has a positive maximizing cycle. Maximality gives the comparison inequality used in subsequent variations.
- **claim-euclidean-current-chart-metrics** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-euclidean-chart-metrics)): For almost every density point $z\in E_i$, the seminorm in <a href="#eq:two-point-metric-differential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:two-point-metric-differential">[eq:two-point-metric-differential]</a> is a Euclidean norm. Thus there is a measurable positive definite matrix $G_i(z)$ with $$\label{eq:chart-metric} N_z(w)^2=w^{\mathsf T}G_i(z)w, \qquad J_i(z)=\sqrt{\det G_i(z)}.$$ In particular, $$\label{eq:centered-metric-differential} d(\phi_i(z+w),\phi_i(z))=\|w\|_{G_i(z)}+o(\|w\|) \quad (z+w\in E_i).$$
  - Explanation: CAT(0) quadrilateral comparison forces the chart metric differential to satisfy the parallelogram law, giving a measurable positive definite inner-product matrix.
- **claim-quadratic-mass-majorant** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-quadratic-mass)): Suppose that a finite-mass $d$-current $C$ has a representation by countably many determinant integrals with Lipschitz maps $\psi_i:F_i\to Y$, Borel sets $F_i\subset\mathbb R^d$, and real signed weights $\vartheta_i$. Suppose that, at almost every point carrying these weights, there is a measurable nonnegative quadratic form $P_i(z)$ such that $$\label{eq:quadratic-distance-majorant} d(\psi_i(z+w),\psi_i(z))^2 \le P_i(z)[w,w]+o(\|w\|^2),\qquad z+w\in F_i.$$ If the measure $$\label{eq:quadratic-mass-majorant} \eta=\sum_i(\psi_i)_\# \bigl(\|\vartheta_i\|\sqrt{\det P_i}\,dz\bigr)$$ is finite, then $\\|C\\|\le\eta$. No normality of the separate chart currents is required.
  - Explanation: A measurable quadratic upper bound for chart distances gives a determinant-weighted upper bound for the current mass measure, including singular forms and nonnormal chart pieces.
- **claim-exact-current-chart-mass** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-chart-mass)): For the representation <a href="#eq:chart-action" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:chart-action">[eq:chart-action]</a>, let $G_i,J_i$ be as in <a href="#eq:chart-metric" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:chart-metric">[eq:chart-metric]</a>. Then $$\label{eq:chart-mass} \\|C\\|=\sum_i(\phi_i)_\#\bigl(\|\theta_i\|J_i\,dz\bigr).$$ Thus, with $\rho_i=\|\theta_i\|J_i$, integration against the current mass is integration against $\rho_i\,dz$ on the disjoint charts.
  - Explanation: The current mass measure equals the sum of chart pushforwards weighted by absolute integer multiplicity and the Euclidean chart Jacobian.
- **claim-euclidean-lower-current-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-euclidean-density)): For an integral $k$-current $C$ in $Y$, $$\label{eq:euclidean-lower-density} \liminf_{\rho\downarrow0} \rho^{-k}\\|C\\|(B(y,\rho))\ge\omega_k \qquad\text{for }\\|C\\|\text{-almost every }y.$$ The balls in this statement are open.
  - Explanation: At almost every mass-carrying point, integer multiplicity and the Euclidean chart metric give the unit-ball lower density coefficient.
- **claim-radial-first-variation** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-radial)): For every center $y\in Y$, write $r(x)=d(y,x)$. Every nonnegative Lipschitz function $g:Y\to\mathbb R$ satisfies $$\int_Y \bigl(ng+r\,Dr\cdot Dg\bigr)\,d\mu \leq n\int_Y rg\sqrt{1-\|Dr\|^2}\,d\mu. \label{eq:radial}$$ The square root is defined $\mu$-almost everywhere by $\|Dr\|\leq 1$.
  - Explanation: For the normalized hypothetical extremizer, radial deformation compares endpoint mass loss with swept filling mass while retaining the transverse square-root factor.
- **claim-sharp-two-cycle-filling** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-base)): Let $Y$ be a compact CAT(0) space. Every integral two-cycle $T\in\mathbf I_2(Y)$ admits $S\in\mathbf I_3(Y)$ with $$\partial S=T,\qquad \mathbf M(S)\leq c_2\mathbf M(T)^{3/2} =\frac{\mathbf M(T)^{3/2}}{6\sqrt{\pi}}.$$
  - Explanation: The induction base follows from an inverse-square radial cutoff and Euclidean lower density, which contradict the normalized small mass of a violating extremizer.
- **claim-uniform-small-set-perimeter** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-small-set)): For every $\eta\in(0,1)$ there is a number $\delta\in(0,m/2)$, independent of the Lipschitz function $u\colon Y\to\mathbb R$, with the following property. Put $$a(t)=\mu\{u>t\},\qquad U_t=A\mathbin{\vrule height 1.4ex depth -0.3ex width .07ex\vrule height .07ex depth 0ex width .7ex}\{u>t\},\qquad P(t)=\mathbf M(\partial U_t),$$ where $U_t$ and its boundary are integral for almost every $t$. At almost every level for which $a(t)\le\delta$, $$P(t)\ge(1-\eta)n\omega_n^{1/n}a(t)^{(n-1)/n}. \label{eq:small-set}$$
  - Explanation: The lower-dimensional sharp filling hypothesis and extremizer maximality produce an almost Euclidean perimeter bound for sufficiently small superlevel restrictions, uniformly in the Lipschitz function.
- **claim-countable-current-mass-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-countable-mass-tests)): Let $Z$ be a nonempty compact metric space and let $k\ge1$. There is a countable collection $\mathcal T_k(Z)$ of finite lists $$\bigl((b_1,\pi^1),\ldots,(b_N,\pi^N)\bigr),$$ where the $b_j$ are Lipschitz, $\sum_j\|b_j\|\le1$ pointwise, and each $\pi^j$ is a $k$-tuple of $1$-Lipschitz functions, such that every $k$-current $C$ of finite mass on $Z$ satisfies $$\mathbf M(C)=\sup_{\mathcal T\in\mathcal T_k(Z)} \sum_{(b,\pi)\in\mathcal T}C(b,\pi). \label{eq:countable-mass-tests}$$ The collection is independent of $C$.
  - Explanation: A current-independent countable collection of admissible finite scalar test lists recovers total mass and permits simultaneous almost-everywhere slice estimates.
- **claim-intrinsic-slice-coarea** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-intrinsic-coarea)): For a real Lipschitz function $u$ on $Y$, put $$a(t)=\mu\{u>t\},\qquad e(t)=\int_{\{u>t\}}\|Du\|^2\,d\mu, \qquad P(t)=\mathbf M\bigl(\partial(A\mathbin{\vrule height 1.4ex depth -0.3ex width .07ex\vrule height .07ex depth 0ex width .7ex}\{u>t\})\bigr)$$ at the almost every level where the restriction is integral. Then $$P(t)^2\le(-a'(t))(-e'(t)),\qquad P(t)\le\operatorname{Lip}(u)(-a'(t)) \quad\text{for almost every }t. \label{eq:intrinsic-coarea}$$ The derivatives are the densities of the absolutely continuous parts of the corresponding distribution measures; neither distribution function is assumed absolutely continuous.
  - Explanation: Slice perimeter is controlled by the absolutely continuous densities of the value and energy distributions. Atoms and singular continuous parts are permitted.
- **claim-rearranged-small-support-energy** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-rearranged-energy)): Fix $\eta\in(0,1)$ and the corresponding $\delta$ from Proposition <a href="#prop:small-set" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:small-set">12</a>. If $u\ge0$ is Lipschitz and $\mu\{u>0\}\le\delta$, there is a nonnegative, radial nonincreasing, compactly supported Lipschitz function $u^*$ on $\mathbb R^n$ such that $$\int_{\mathbb R^n}(u^*)^q\,dx=\int_Yu^q\,d\mu\quad(q>0), \qquad \int_{\mathbb R^n}\|\nabla u^*\|^2\,dx \le(1-\eta)^{-2}E(u). \label{eq:rearranged-energy-bound}$$
  - Explanation: The generalized inverse of the superlevel-radius distribution constructs a radial Lipschitz rearrangement preserving all positive moments and controlling energy despite singular distribution parts.
- **claim-sharp-radial-euclidean-sobolev** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-radial-sobolev)): For every nonnegative, radial nonincreasing, compactly supported Lipschitz function $f$ on $\mathbb R^n$, where $n>2$, $$ns_n^{2/n} \left(\int_{\mathbb R^n}f^p\,dx\right)^{2/p} \le \frac4{n-2}\int_{\mathbb R^n}\|\nabla f\|^2\,dx. \label{eq:radial-sobolev}$$
  - Explanation: Radial mass transport to a truncated Euclidean comparison profile proves the sharp critical Sobolev coefficient for the stated radial class.
- **claim-global-almost-sharp-sobolev** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-almost-sobolev)): For every $\epsilon\in(0,S_*)$ there is a finite constant $C_\epsilon\ge0$ such that every real Lipschitz function $u$ on $Y$ satisfies $$(S_*-\epsilon)\\|u\\|_p^2 \le4\beta E(u)+C_\epsilon\\|u\\|_2^2. \label{eq:almost-sobolev}$$ The constant is independent of $u$.
  - Explanation: Level truncation extends the small-support estimate to every real Lipschitz function, with an arbitrarily small loss in the sharp coefficient and a lower-order term.
- **claim-closed-sobolev-gradient-calculus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-sobolev-space)): Identify real Lipschitz functions on $Y$ that agree $\mu$-almost everywhere. Their completion in the norm $$\\|u\\|_{\mathcal H}^2=\\|u\\|_2^2+E(u)$$ is a Hilbert space $\mathcal H$ of functions in $L^2(\mu)$. Its gradient is a well-defined closed operator with values in the Hilbert space of square-integrable chart covectors. The inclusion $\mathcal H\mathrel{\begingroup \let\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\endcsname \let\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\endcsname \let\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\endcsname \let\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname \let\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\endcsname \let\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\endcsname \pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}% PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\endgroup}L^p(\mu)$ is continuous, and <a href="#eq:almost-sobolev" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:almost-sobolev">[eq:almost-sobolev]</a> holds for all $u\in\mathcal H$.* *The following calculus properties hold.* 1. *If $u_1,\ldots,u_k\in\mathcal H$ and $\Phi\in C^1(\mathbb R^k)$ has bounded first derivatives, then $\Phi(u_1,\ldots,u_k)\in\mathcal H$ and $$D\Phi(u_1,\ldots,u_k) =\sum_{a=1}^k\partial_a\Phi(u_1,\ldots,u_k)Du_a.$$* 2. *For every $u\in\mathcal H$ and every fixed $t\in\mathbb R$, $Du=0$ almost everywhere on $\{u=t\}$. Consequently, the scalar chain rule also holds for Lipschitz, piecewise $C^1$ functions with finitely many corners, with any derivative values assigned at the corners.* 3. *The map $u\mathrel{\begingroup \let\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\endcsname \let\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\endcsname \let\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\endcsname \let\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname \let\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\endcsname \let\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\endcsname \pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF21A6> >> BDC}% PPaperOriginalmapsto\endcsname\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\endgroup}u_+$ is continuous from $\mathcal H$ to itself. Every nonnegative element of $\mathcal H$ has nonnegative Lipschitz approximants converging in $\mathcal H$.* 4. *For each fixed center $y\in Y$, the radial inequality <a href="#eq:radial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial">[eq:radial]</a> holds for every nonnegative $g\in\mathcal H$, with $r=d(y,\cdot)$ and its chart gradient $Dr$.
  - Explanation: Closing the chart gradient gives a Hilbert space of functions, a continuous critical embedding, chain rules, level-set locality, positive approximation, and extended radial tests.
- **claim-compact-sobolev-inclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-compact-embedding)): The inclusion $\mathcal H\mathrel{\begingroup \let\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\endcsname \let\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\endcsname \let\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\endcsname \let\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname \let\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\endcsname \let\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\endcsname \pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}% PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\endgroup}L^2(\mu)$ is compact.
  - Explanation: Projection of whole weighted currents, translation compactness, chart isolation in total variation, and interpolation establish compactness of the inclusion.
- **claim-subthreshold-normalized-minimizer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-minimizer)): One has $0<\Lambda<S_*$. There exists a nonzero nonnegative $v\in\mathcal H$ minimizing $Q(u)/\\|u\\|_p^2$ over nonzero $u\in\mathcal H$ and satisfying $$\begin{split} 0<W:=\int v^p\,d\mu =\left(\frac\Lambda n\right)^{n/2}<s_n, \qquad Q(v)=nW. \end{split} \label{eq:minimizer-normalization}$$ For every $\psi\in\mathcal H$ it satisfies the weak equation $$4\beta\int Dv\cdot D\psi\,d\mu+n\int v\psi\,d\mu =n\int v^{p-1}\psi\,d\mu. \label{eq:euler}$$
  - Explanation: The quotient infimum lies strictly below the critical threshold. Compactness and critical mass splitting yield a nonnegative minimizer with the specified normalization and weak Euler equation.
- **claim-minimizer-powers-and-weighted-energy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-powers)): For every real $\gamma\ge1$, $$v^\gamma\in\mathcal H\cap L^p(\mu),\qquad D(v^\gamma)=\gamma v^{\gamma-1}Dv.$$ Moreover, $\int v^q\,d\mu<\infty$ for every finite $q>0$, and $$\int v^q\|Dv\|^2\,d\mu<\infty \qquad\hbox{for every finite }q\ge0. \label{eq:polynomial-weighted-energy}$$
  - Explanation: Truncated-power tests and critical-potential tail absorption give all finite positive moments and polynomially weighted energies of the normalized minimizer.
- **claim-polynomial-composite-chain-rule** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-composites)): Let $r\in\mathcal H$ satisfy $0\le r\le R<\infty$ almost everywhere and $\|Dr\|\in L^\infty(\mu)$. Suppose that $F$ is $C^1$ on an open neighborhood of $[0,R]\times[0,\infty)$ and that, on this strip, $$\|F(a,t)\|+\|\partial_1F(a,t)\|+\|\partial_2F(a,t)\| \le C(1+t)^M$$ for some finite $C$ and nonnegative integer $M$. Then $F(r,v)\in\mathcal H$ and $$D(F(r,v))=\partial_1F(r,v)Dr+\partial_2F(r,v)Dv. \label{eq:polynomial-composite-chain-rule}$$ In particular, this applies with $r=d(y,\cdot)$ for each fixed $y\in Y$.
  - Explanation: Polynomial moment and weighted-energy bounds allow a chain rule for the actual composite of a bounded function with bounded gradient and the minimizer.
- **claim-admissible-chord-composites** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-chord-tests)): Fix $a>0$ and put $$w(t)=(1+at^2)^{-(n-2)/2}\qquad(t\in\mathbb R).$$ Then $vw(s)$ and $vw(s)^2$ belong to $\mathcal H$, and $$\label{chord:product-rules} \begin{split} D(vw(s))&=w(s)Dv+w'(s)\xi,\\ D(vw(s)^2)&=w(s)^2Dv+2w(s)w'(s)\xi. \end{split}$$ If $f:\mathbb R\to[0,\infty)$ is smooth with bounded value and bounded first derivative, then $$\label{chord:radial-tests} g=\kappa^2v^{2+2\beta}f(s),\qquad \psi=\beta\kappa^2r^2v^{1+2\beta}f(s)$$ also belong to $\mathcal H$. In particular, $g$ is an admissible nonnegative test in the extended radial inequality, and $\psi$ is an admissible test in <a href="#eq:euler" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:euler">[eq:euler]</a>.
  - Explanation: The genuine composite test functions belong to the completed Sobolev space and satisfy their product rules even on the zero set of the minimizer.
- **claim-completed-radial-square-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-completed-radial)): For every center $y\in Y$, with $r=d(y,\cdot)$, and every nonnegative $g\in\mathcal H$, $$\label{chord:completed-radial} \int_Y\bigl(ng\lvert Dr\rvert^2+r\langle Dr,Dg\rangle\bigr)\,d\mu \le\frac n4\int_Y r^2g\,d\mu.$$
  - Explanation: Completing a square in the extended radial inequality bounds the radial gradient expression by a quadratic distance moment.
- **claim-chord-distribution-transform-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-chord-transform)): For every center $y$ with $v(y)>0$, and every $a\ge0$, $$\label{eq:chord-transform} J_y(a)=\int_Y(1+as^2)^{-n}\,d\nu \le(1+2aL_y)^{-n/2},$$ where $s$ and $L_y$ are defined in <a href="#chord:data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="chord:data">[chord:data]</a>.
  - Explanation: Quotient minimality, admissible chord tests, and radial variation give a scalar differential inequality controlling the chord transform by its second moment.
- **claim-chord-tangent-density-and-strict-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-chord-density)): For $\nu$-almost every center $y$, $$\label{chord:transform-density} \liminf_{a\to\infty}a^{n/2}J_y(a) \ge\frac{s_n}{2^nW}.$$ Consequently, $$\label{eq:chord-upper} L_y\le2\left(\frac{W}{s_n}\right)^{2/n}<2 \qquad\text{for $\nu$-almost every }y.$$
  - Explanation: Rescaling at chart Lebesgue points and using integer multiplicity gives a lower transform coefficient. Comparison with the transform upper bound forces the strict chord-moment estimate.
- **claim-opposite-averaged-chord-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-opposite-average)): Define $$\label{finish:weighted-moment} B_*:=\int_Y v^{p+2\beta}\,d\mu, \qquad I(y):=\int_Y d(y,x)^2v(x)^{p+2\beta}\,d\mu(x).$$ Then $0<B_*<\infty$, the function $I$ is finite and continuous on $Y$, and $$\label{finish:all-centers} W^2\le B_*I(y)\qquad\text{for every }y\in Y.$$ In particular, for the chord moments $$L_y=\int_Y \bigl[v(y)^\beta d(y,x)v(x)^\beta\bigr]^2\,d\nu(x),$$ one has $$\label{finish:opposite-average} \int_Y L_y\,d\nu(y)\ge2.$$
  - Explanation: Radial testing and the Euler equation give an all-center distance-moment estimate. CAT(0) barycenter variance then forces an opposite lower bound for the averaged chord moment.
- **claim-compact-top-dimensional-cycle-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-top-dimensional-constancy)): Let $M$ be a connected, noncompact, oriented Riemannian manifold of dimension $d$. If $C\in\mathbf I_d(M)$ has compact support and $\partial C=0$, then $C=0$.
  - Explanation: A boundary-free top-dimensional integral current has locally constant multiplicity. Connectedness and compact support in a noncompact manifold force that multiplicity to vanish.

Zero multiplicities may be discarded. Scalar functions on a Borel chart domain are differentiated by Lipschitz extension to $\\mathbb R^k$; their derivatives are independent of that extension at almost every density point of the domain.*\n\n\n4.  *For $h>0$, the whole-current product $[0,h]\\times C$, with the Euclidean product metric, is an integral $(k+1)$-current and obeys $$\\label{eq:interval-product-boundary}\n     \\partial([0,h]\\times C)\n     =[h]\\times C-[0]\\times C-[0,h]\\times\\partial C.$$ Writing $b_t(x)=b(t,x)$ and $\\pi_{a,t}(x)=\\pi_a(t,x)$, its action is $$\\label{eq:interval-product-action}\n    \\begin{split}\n     &([0,h]\\times C)(b,\\pi_1,\\ldots,\\pi_{k+1})\\\\\n     &\\quad=\\sum_{a=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{a+1}\\int_0^h\n     C\\bigl(b_t\\,\\partial_t\\pi_a(t,\\cdot),\n              \\pi_{1,t},\\ldots,\\widehat{\\pi_{a,t}},\\ldots,\n              \\pi_{k+1,t}\\bigr)\\,dt.\n    \\end{split}$$ The hat indicates omission. The time derivative is interpreted almost everywhere and as a bounded Borel first coefficient in the current action.*\n\n\n5.  *There are finite constants $K_k,L_k$, independent of the cycle, such that every integral $k$-cycle $B$ in $Y$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:coarse-fillings}\n     V(B)\\le K_k\\mathbf M(B)^{(k+1)/k},\\qquad\n     V(B)\\le L_k\\mathbf M(B).$$ Every such $B$ has a mass-minimizing integral filling in $Y$. If integral $k$-cycles $B_j$ have bounded masses and converge weakly to an integral cycle $B$, then $$\\label{eq:filling-continuity}\n     V(B_j-B)\\mathrel{\\begingroup\n      \\let\\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}%\n      PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\\endgroup}0,\n     \\qquad V(B_j)\\mathrel{\\begingroup\n      \\let\\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}%\n      PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\\endgroup}V(B).$$",

          "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be compact and $\\operatorname{CAT}(0)$, and let $k\\ge1$. The following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *Lipschitz pushforward preserves integral currents, commutes with boundary, and satisfies the Lipschitz mass bound. Restrictions by bounded Borel coefficients have the usual mass-measure bound; for a Borel set $E\\subset Y$, $$\\|C\\mathbin{\\vrule height 1.4ex depth -0.3ex width .07ex\\vrule height .07ex depth 0ex width .7ex}E\\|=\\|C\\|\\mathbin{\\vrule height 1.4ex depth -0.3ex width .07ex\\vrule height .07ex depth 0ex width .7ex}E.$$ The restricted current need not be normal. If $u:Y\\to\\mathbb R$ is Lipschitz and $C\\in\\mathbf I_k(Y)$, then $C\\mathbin{\\vrule height 1.4ex depth -0.3ex width .07ex\\vrule height .07ex depth 0ex width .7ex}\\{u>t\\}$ is integral for almost every $t$. In particular its boundary is an integral $(k-1)$-cycle.*\n\n2.  *If $C_j\\in\\mathbf I_k(Y)$ and $\\sup_j(\\mathbf M(C_j)+\\mathbf M(\\partial C_j))<\\infty$, a subsequence converges weakly to an integral current. Boundary commutes with weak convergence, and mass is lower semicontinuous.*\n\n\n3.  *Every $C\\in\\mathbf I_k(Y)$ admits a representation by bi-Lipschitz maps $\\phi_i:E_i\\to Y$, where $E_i\\subset\\mathbb R^k$ are bounded Borel sets and the images $Z_i=\\phi_i(E_i)$ are pairwise disjoint Borel sets, and by signed integer multiplicities $\\theta_i\\in L^1(E_i)$, such that $$\\label{eq:chart-action}\n     C(b,\\pi_1,\\ldots,\\pi_k)\n     =\\sum_i\\int_{E_i}\\theta_i(z)b(\\phi_i(z))\n           \\det D\\bigl((\\pi_1,\\ldots,\\pi_k)\\circ\\phi_i\\bigr)(z)\\,dz.$$ The chart summands have summable masses. Zero multiplicities may be discarded. Scalar functions on a Borel chart domain are differentiated by Lipschitz extension to $\\mathbb R^k$; their derivatives are independent of that extension at almost every density point of the domain.*\n\n\n4.  *For $h>0$, the whole-current product $[0,h]\\times C$, with the Euclidean product metric, is an integral $(k+1)$-current and obeys $$\\label{eq:interval-product-boundary}\n     \\partial([0,h]\\times C)\n     =[h]\\times C-[0]\\times C-[0,h]\\times\\partial C.$$ Writing $b_t(x)=b(t,x)$ and $\\pi_{a,t}(x)=\\pi_a(t,x)$, its action is $$\\label{eq:interval-product-action}\n    \\begin{split}\n     &([0,h]\\times C)(b,\\pi_1,\\ldots,\\pi_{k+1})\\\\\n     &\\quad=\\sum_{a=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{a+1}\\int_0^h\n     C\\bigl(b_t\\,\\partial_t\\pi_a(t,\\cdot),\n              \\pi_{1,t},\\ldots,\\widehat{\\pi_{a,t}},\\ldots,\n              \\pi_{k+1,t}\\bigr)\\,dt.\n    \\end{split}$$ The hat indicates omission. The time derivative is interpreted almost everywhere and as a bounded Borel first coefficient in the current action.*\n\n\n5.  *There are finite constants $K_k,L_k$, independent of the cycle, such that every integral $k$-cycle $B$ in $Y$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:coarse-fillings}\n     V(B)\\le K_k\\mathbf M(B)^{(k+1)/k},\\qquad\n     V(B)\\le L_k\\mathbf M(B).$$ Every such $B$ has a mass-minimizing integral filling in $Y$. If integral $k$-cycles $B_j$ have bounded masses and converge weakly to an integral cycle $B$, then $$\\label{eq:filling-continuity}\n     V(B_j-B)\\mathrel{\\begingroup\n      \\let\\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}%\n      PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\\endgroup}0,\n     \\qquad V(B_j)\\mathrel{\\begingroup\n      \\let\\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}%\n      PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\\endgroup}V(B).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-337.2"
        }
      ],

If integral $k$-cycles $B_j$ have bounded masses and converge weakly to an integral cycle $B$, then $$\\label{eq:filling-continuity}\n     V(B_j-B)\\mathrel{\\begingroup\n      \\let\\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}%\n      PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\\endgroup}0,\n     \\qquad V(B_j)\\mathrel{\\begingroup\n      \\let\\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}%\n      PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\\endgroup}V(B).$$",

      },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-extremizer",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-euclidean-chart-metrics",
        "exact_quote": "For almost every density point $z\\in E_i$, the seminorm in <a href=\"#eq:two-point-metric-differential\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:two-point-metric-differential\">[eq:two-point-metric-differential]</a> is a Euclidean norm. Thus there is a measurable positive definite matrix $G_i(z)$ with $$\\label{eq:chart-metric}\n N_z(w)^2=w^{\\mathsf T}G_i(z)w,\n \\qquad J_i(z)=\\sqrt{\\det G_i(z)}.$$ In particular, $$\\label{eq:centered-metric-differential}\n d(\\phi_i(z+w),\\phi_i(z))=|w|_{G_i(z)}+o(|w|)\n \\quad (z+w\\in E_i).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-337.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-euclidean-chart-metrics",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-countable-mass-tests",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a nonempty compact metric space and let $k\\ge1$.       },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-sobolev-space",
        "exact_quote": "Identify real Lipschitz functions on $Y$ that agree $\\mu$-almost everywhere. Their completion in the norm $$\\|u\\|_{\\mathcal H}^2=\\|u\\|_2^2+E(u)$$ is a Hilbert space $\\mathcal H$ of functions in $L^2(\\mu)$. Its gradient is a well-defined closed operator with values in the Hilbert space of square-integrable chart covectors. The inclusion $\\mathcal H\\mathrel{\\begingroup\n  \\let\\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\let\\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\n  \\let\\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\\endcsname\n  \\let\\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\let\\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\let\\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}%\n  PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\\endgroup}L^p(\\mu)$ is continuous, and <a href=\"#eq:almost-sobolev\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:almost-sobolev\">[eq:almost-sobolev]</a> holds for all $u\\in\\mathcal H$.*\n\n*The following calculus properties hold.*\n\n1.  *If $u_1,\\ldots,u_k\\in\\mathcal H$ and $\\Phi\\in C^1(\\mathbb R^k)$ has bounded first derivatives, then $\\Phi(u_1,\\ldots,u_k)\\in\\mathcal H$ and $$D\\Phi(u_1,\\ldots,u_k)\n     =\\sum_{a=1}^k\\partial_a\\Phi(u_1,\\ldots,u_k)Du_a.$$*\n\n2.  *For every $u\\in\\mathcal H$ and every fixed $t\\in\\mathbb R$, $Du=0$ almost everywhere on $\\{u=t\\}$. Consequently, the scalar chain rule also holds for Lipschitz, piecewise $C^1$ functions with finitely many corners, with any derivative values assigned at the corners.*\n\n3.  *The map $u\\mathrel{\\begingroup\n      \\let\\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF21A6> >> BDC}%\n      PPaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\\endgroup}u_+$ is continuous from $\\mathcal H$ to itself. Every nonnegative element of $\\mathcal H$ has nonnegative Lipschitz approximants converging in $\\mathcal H$.*\n\n4.  *For each fixed center $y\\in Y$, the radial inequality <a href=\"#eq:radial\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:radial\">[eq:radial]</a> holds for every nonnegative $g\\in\\mathcal H$, with $r=d(y,\\cdot)$ and its chart gradient $Dr$.",
        "source_id": "PM-337.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "One has $0<\\Lambda<S_*$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-composites",
        "exact_quote": "Then $F(r,v)\\in\\mathcal H$ and $$D(F(r,v))=\\partial_1F(r,v)Dr+\\partial_2F(r,v)Dv.\n \\label{eq:polynomial-composite-chain-rule}$$ In particular, this applies with $r=d(y,\\cdot)$ for each fixed $y\\in Y$.",

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-composites",
          "exact_quote": "Let $r\\in\\mathcal H$ satisfy $0\\le r\\le R<\\infty$ almost everywhere and $|Dr|\\in L^\\infty(\\mu)$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-chord-tests",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $a>0$ and put $$w(t)=(1+at^2)^{-(n-2)/2}\\qquad(t\\in\\mathbb R).$$ Then $vw(s)$ and $vw(s)^2$ belong to $\\mathcal H$, and $$\\label{chord:product-rules}\n \\begin{split}\n D(vw(s))&=w(s)Dv+w'(s)\\xi,\\\\\n D(vw(s)^2)&=w(s)^2Dv+2w(s)w'(s)\\xi.\n \\end{split}$$ If $f:\\mathbb R\\to[0,\\infty)$ is smooth with bounded value and bounded first derivative, then $$\\label{chord:radial-tests}\n g=\\kappa^2v^{2+2\\beta}f(s),\\qquad\n \\psi=\\beta\\kappa^2r^2v^{1+2\\beta}f(s)$$ also belong to $\\mathcal H$.         "source_id": "PM-337.2"
      },

      "exact_statement": "Fix $a>0$ and put $$w(t)=(1+at^2)^{-(n-2)/2}\\qquad(t\\in\\mathbb R).$$ Then $vw(s)$ and $vw(s)^2$ belong to $\\mathcal H$, and $$\\label{chord:product-rules}\n \\begin{split}\n D(vw(s))&=w(s)Dv+w'(s)\\xi,\\\\\n D(vw(s)^2)&=w(s)^2Dv+2w(s)w'(s)\\xi.\n \\end{split}$$ If $f:\\mathbb R\\to[0,\\infty)$ is smooth with bounded value and bounded first derivative, then $$\\label{chord:radial-tests}\n g=\\kappa^2v^{2+2\\beta}f(s),\\qquad\n \\psi=\\beta\\kappa^2r^2v^{1+2\\beta}f(s)$$ also belong to $\\mathcal H$.       "explanation": "The genuine composite test functions belong to the completed Sobolev space and satisfy their product rules even on the zero set of the minimizer.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Integral cycle and integral filling** ([source](#sec:introduction)): An integral $n$-cycle is a current $T\in\mathbf I_n(X)$ with $\partial T=0$; an integral filling is a current $S\in\mathbf I_{n+1}(X)$ with $\partial S=T$.
- **Proper metric space** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A metric space is *proper* if its closed bounded balls are compact.
- **CAT(0) space** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A CAT(0) space is a geodesic metric space in which the distance between two points on a geodesic triangle is at most the distance between the corresponding points of its Euclidean comparison triangle.
- **Euclidean sphere area and filling coefficient** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Let $\omega_j$ be the volume of the unit ball in $\mathbb R^j$ and put $$\label{eq:constants} s_n=(n+1)\omega_{n+1}=\operatorname{area}(\mathbb S^n), \qquad c_n=\frac{1}{(n+1)s_n^{1/n}}.$$
- **Cartan–Hadamard manifold** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A *Cartan–Hadamard manifold* is a complete, simply connected Riemannian manifold of nonpositive sectional curvature.
- **Least integral filling mass** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For an integral $k$-cycle $B$ in $Y$, define $$\label{eq:filling-volume-definition} V(B)=\inf\{\mathbf M(S):S\in\mathbf I_{k+1}(Y),\ \partial S=B\}.$$
- **Current mass measure and total mass** ([source](#eq:mass-controlling-measure)): The mass measure of a current $C$ is denoted by $\\|C\\|$, and its total mass by $\mathbf M(C)$.
- **Euclidean chart metric and Jacobian** ([source](#stmt-lem-euclidean-chart-metrics)): Thus there is a measurable positive definite matrix $G_i(z)$ with $$\label{eq:chart-metric} N_z(w)^2=w^{\mathsf T}G_i(z)w, \qquad J_i(z)=\sqrt{\det G_i(z)}.$$
- **Chart differential** ([source](#eq:gradient-lipschitz)): For a Lipschitz scalar function $u$ on $Y$, define its chart covector $Du$ by differentiating $u\circ\phi_i$ and transporting that covector to $Z_i$.
- **Sobolev parameters and Dirichlet energy** ([source](#sec:sobolev)): Set $$\beta=\frac1{n-2},\qquad p=\frac{2n}{n-2},\qquad S_*=ns_n^{2/n},\qquad E(u)=\int_Y\|Du\|^2\,d\mu. \label{eq:sobolev-notation}$$
- **Completed Sobolev space** ([source](#stmt-prop-sobolev-space)): Their completion in the norm $$\\|u\\|_{\mathcal H}^2=\\|u\\|_2^2+E(u)$$ is a Hilbert space $\mathcal H$ of functions in $L^2(\mu)$.
- **Critical quadratic functional and constrained infimum** ([source](#pm-h-21)): Define the quadratic functional and its critical constrained infimum by $$Q(u)=4\beta E(u)+n\\|u\\|_2^2, \qquad \Lambda=\inf\{Q(u):u\in\mathcal H,\ \\|u\\|_p=1\}. \label{eq:critical-variational-problem}$$
- **Conformally weighted chord and its second moment** ([source](#chord:data)): For a fixed center $y\in Y$ with $v(y)>0$, set $$\label{chord:data} \kappa=v(y)^\beta,\qquad r(x)=d(y,x),\qquad s(x)=\kappa r(x)v(x)^\beta, \qquad L_y=\int_Y s^2\,d\nu.$$
- **Chord distribution transform** ([source](#sec:chords)): We study the chord distribution through the transform $$\label{chord:transform-definition} J_y(a)=\int_Y(1+as^2)^{-n}\,d\nu\qquad(a\ge0).$$
- **Weighted chord covector** ([source](#chord:xi)): Instead, define the square-integrable chart covector field $$\label{chord:xi} \xi=\kappa\bigl(v^{1+\beta}Dr +\beta r v^\beta Dv\bigr).$$

| q-normalized-extremizer-mass | m | = | $((n+1)c)^{-n}$ | A constant metric rescaling preserves maximality. |

| q-small-set-perimeter | P(t) | ≥ | $(1-\eta)n\omega_n^{1/n}a(t)^{(n-1)/n}$ | The small-mass threshold is independent of the Lipschitz function. |
| q-critical-exponent | p | = | $\frac{2n}{n-2}$ | Higher-dimensional induction. |
| q-sobolev-weight | \beta | = | $\frac1{n-2}$ | Higher-dimensional cycle dimension. |

| q-rearranged-energy-loss | \int_{\mathbb R^n}\|\nabla u^*\|^2\,dx | ≤ | $(1-\eta)^{-2}E(u)$ | The loss parameter and small-set threshold are fixed together. |
| q-stereographic-profile-integral | I_0 | = | $2^{-n}s_n$ | The integral is the comparison profile normalization. |
| q-minimizer-weighted-mass | W | = | $\left(\frac\Lambda n\right)^{n/2}$ | The weighted mass is positive and strictly below sphere area. |
| q-chord-transform-upper-bound | J_y(a) | ≤ | $(1+2aL_y)^{-n/2}$ | An eligible fixed center and a nonnegative transform parameter. |
| q-chord-transform-tangent-coefficient | \liminf_{a\to\infty}a^{n/2}J_y(a) | ≥ | $\frac{s_n}{2^nW}$ | The transform parameter tends to infinity. |
| q-strict-chord-second-moment | L_y | ≤ | $2\left(\frac{W}{s_n}\right)^{2/n}$ | The normalized weighted mass is strictly subthreshold. |
| q-opposite-average-lower-bound | \int_Y L_y\,d\nu(y) | ≥ | $2$ | The probability measure and minimizer are those of the contradiction argument. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-compact-reduction** [claim](#stmt-lem-compact-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-compact-reduction): Enclose the support in a compact convex ball, apply nonexpanding radial projection, and include the compact filling. -> A compactly supported filling in the original space with no increase of the bound.
- **step-current-foundations** [claim](#stmt-thm-current-background) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-current-background): Combine integral-current calculus and compactness with geodesic cone inequalities and weak-to-filling continuity. -> Finite attained filling volumes, weak compactness, slicing, and whole-current product identities.
- **step-positive-extremizer** [claim](#stmt-lem-extremizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-extremizer): Bound the masses in a positive maximizing sequence, take a weak limit, and use filling continuity and mass lower semicontinuity. -> A nonzero maximizing cycle and its comparison inequality.
- **step-euclidean-chart-metrics** [claim](#stmt-lem-euclidean-chart-metrics) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-euclidean-chart-metrics): Approximate finite domain configurations at density points and derive the parallelogram law from CAT(0) quadrilateral comparison. -> Measurable positive definite chart metric matrices.
- **step-quadratic-mass-upper-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-quadratic-mass) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-quadratic-mass): Bound scalar derivative rows by the quadratic form, apply the determinant inequality, and use minimality of the mass measure. -> An upper bound for the current mass measure without normality of individual chart pieces.
- **step-exact-chart-mass** [claim](#stmt-prop-chart-mass) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-chart-mass): Use almost isometric chart partitions and extended inverse coordinates for the lower mass estimate, then apply the quadratic majorant for the reverse estimate. -> Exact chart mass with absolute signed multiplicities.
- **step-euclidean-lower-density** [claim](#stmt-lem-euclidean-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-euclidean-density): Map infinitesimal chart ellipsoids into open metric balls and apply Lebesgue differentiation with nonzero integer multiplicity. -> The Euclidean unit-ball lower density at almost every mass-carrying point.
- **step-radial-first-variation** [claim](#stmt-prop-radial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-radial): Compare unequal radial fractions, compute spatial and swept quadratic determinants, bound whole-current masses, and pass through extremizer maximality. -> The sharp radial first-variation inequality retaining the transverse factor.
- **step-two-cycle-base** [claim](#stmt-prop-base) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-base): Insert an inverse-square distance cutoff, exclude positive-mass separating levels, and compare the limiting estimate with Euclidean lower density. -> The sharp filling theorem in the base cycle dimension.
- **step-small-set-replacement** [claim](#stmt-prop-small-set) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-small-set): Fill a superlevel restriction boundary using the lower-dimensional induction hypothesis and compare the replacement cycle through maximality. -> A uniform almost Euclidean small-set perimeter estimate.
- **step-countable-mass-tests** [claim](#stmt-lem-countable-mass-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-countable-mass-tests): Choose dense normalized differentiated tuples, recover mass by signed densities, and approximate admissible finite coefficient lists. -> A current-independent countable family determining total mass.
- **step-intrinsic-coarea** [claim](#stmt-lem-intrinsic-coarea) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-intrinsic-coarea): Integrate slice evaluations using the whole-cycle product rule, differentiate simultaneously over countably many lists, and apply Cauchy–Schwarz to pushforward measures. -> Slice-perimeter bounds using absolutely continuous distribution densities.
- **step-generalized-inverse-rearrangement** [claim](#stmt-lem-rearranged-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rearranged-energy): Construct the generalized inverse of the superlevel-radius distribution and use exact inverse substitution for energy. -> A radial equimeasurable Lipschitz function with controlled energy, including atomic and singular distributions.
- **step-radial-sharp-sobolev** [claim](#stmt-lem-radial-sobolev) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-radial-sobolev): Match cumulative radial masses to a truncated comparison profile, compare determinant and trace, integrate by parts, and compute the limiting profile energy stereographically. -> The sharp radial Euclidean Sobolev coefficient.
- **step-global-sobolev-truncation** [claim](#stmt-prop-almost-sobolev) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-almost-sobolev): Combine rearrangement and radial Sobolev for small supports, then truncate at a level controlled by the lower norm. -> An almost sharp global critical inequality with a function-independent lower-order constant.
- **step-close-gradient-and-calculus** [claim](#stmt-prop-sobolev-space) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-sobolev-space): Prove closability by whole-cycle signed-measure tests before chart localization, then extend the embedding and calculus through strong graph convergence. -> The Hilbert Sobolev space, closed gradient, chain rules, positive approximation, and extended radial inequality.
- **step-compact-sobolev-embedding** [claim](#stmt-prop-compact-embedding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-compact-embedding): Project whole weighted currents, obtain translation compactness of signed measures, isolate charts in total variation, and assemble with interpolation. -> Strong subsequential convergence in the lower function norm.
- **step-subthreshold-minimizer** [claim](#stmt-prop-minimizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-minimizer): Test with a constant to obtain strict subthreshold energy, split critical mass along a minimizing sequence, exclude mass loss, and normalize the Euler equation. -> A nonnegative normalized minimizer with positive subthreshold weighted mass.
- **step-minimizer-power-bootstrap** [claim](#stmt-lem-powers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-powers): Use truncated powers in the Euler equation, absorb the critical-potential tail, iterate moment improvement, and pass strongly through closedness. -> All finite positive moments, admissible powers, and polynomial weighted energies.
- **step-polynomial-composite-calculus** [claim](#stmt-lem-composites) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-composites): Truncate the minimizer, extend the composite smoothly on compact rectangles, and pass by polynomial domination and closedness. -> Admissibility and the chain rule for polynomial-growth composites.
- **step-chord-test-admissibility** [claim](#stmt-lem-chord-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chord-tests): Extend the actual composite tests differentiably across the zero set and apply the polynomial composite calculus. -> Admissible quotient, Euler, and radial chord tests with valid product formulas.
- **step-completed-radial-square** [claim](#stmt-lem-completed-radial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-completed-radial): Complete the square in the radial gradient and combine it with the extended radial inequality. -> The quadratic radial estimate used in the chord energy calculation.
- **step-chord-transform-comparison** [claim](#stmt-prop-chord-transform) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-chord-transform): Combine the ground-state identity, quotient minimality, and added radial–Euler tests, then integrate the scalar differential inequality from its initial derivative. -> The transform upper bound at every eligible fixed center.
- **step-chord-tangent-density** [claim](#stmt-lem-chord-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-chord-density): Rescale chart Lebesgue points, pass by Fatou, cancel conformal powers in a linear substitution, and use nonzero integer multiplicity. -> A tangent transform lower coefficient and the strict almost-everywhere chord-moment upper bound.
- **step-opposite-chord-average** [claim](#stmt-lem-opposite-average) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-opposite-average): Test radial variation by a critical power, factor and apply Cauchy–Schwarz, identify the energy using the Euler equation, and average CAT(0) barycenter variance. -> An all-center weighted moment estimate and the opposite averaged chord lower bound.
- **step-complete-sharp-filling-induction** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-opposite-average-2): Contradict the averaged lower bound with the strict chord upper bound, complete the dimension induction, invoke attainment, and transfer through compact reduction. -> Attained compactly supported sharp integral fillings in every allowed cycle dimension.
- **step-top-dimensional-constancy** [claim](#stmt-lem-top-dimensional-constancy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-top-dimensional-constancy): Test the boundary in oriented coordinate balls, deduce constant multiplicity from vanishing distributional derivatives, and use connectedness and support. -> Vanishing of the compactly supported top-dimensional cycle.
- **step-domain-corollary-and-sharpness** [claim](#stmt-cor-cartan-hadamard) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-top-dimensional-constancy-2): Apply the filling theorem to the domain boundary and use top-dimensional constancy to identify its filling; Euclidean balls demonstrate coefficient optimality. -> The smooth and finite-perimeter domain inequalities and optimality of the universal filling coefficient.

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-minimizer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $u_j$ be an $L^p$-normalized minimizing sequence. It is bounded in $\\mathcal H$, since $Q$ is a positive definite quadratic form equivalent to the squared graph norm. By weak compactness in a Hilbert space and Proposition <a href=\"#prop:compact-embedding\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:compact-embedding\">19</a>, after taking a subsequence we have $$u_j\\rightharpoonup u\\quad\\hbox{in }\\mathcal H,\n \\qquad u_j\\to u\\quad\\hbox{in }L^2(\\mu)\n \\quad\\hbox{and almost everywhere}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-337.2"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "Existence uses weak Hilbert-space compactness, strong subsequential convergence, and exclusion of critical mass loss.",
      "step_id": "step-subthreshold-minimizer"
    },
    {
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-opposite-average-2",
          "exact_quote": "For the probability measure $\\nu=v^p\\mu/W$, the chord upper bound <a href=\"#eq:chord-upper\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:chord-upper\">[eq:chord-upper]</a> holds for $\\nu$-almost every center. Integrating that bound and using Lemma <a href=\"#lem:opposite-average\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:opposite-average\">28</a> gives $$2\\le\\int_YL_y\\,d\\nu(y)\n   \\le2\\left(\\frac W{s_n}\\right)^{2/n}<2,$$ a contradiction.",
          "source_id": "PM-337.2"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The main existence result excludes a hypothetical violating extremizer by contradictory chord bounds and then invokes variational attainment.",
      "step_id": "step-complete-sharp-filling-induction"
    }
  ],
  "pm_id": "PM-337.2",

  "steps": [
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-compact-reduction",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-compact-reduction",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that the asserted filling inequality in a fixed cycle dimension holds in every compact $\\operatorname{CAT}(0)$ space. Then it holds for every compactly supported integral cycle of that dimension in every proper $\\operatorname{CAT}(0)$ space, with a compactly supported integral filling.",
          "source_id": "PM-337.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-compact-reduction",
      "inputs": [
        "Proper CAT(0) ambient space",
        "Compactly supported integral cycle",
        "Compact-space filling inequality"
      ],
      "method": "Enclose the support in a compact convex ball, apply nonexpanding radial projection, and include the compact filling.",
      "output": "A compactly supported filling in the original space with no increase of the bound.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-compact-reduction"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-thm-current-background",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-current-background",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be compact and $\\operatorname{CAT}(0)$, and let $k\\ge1$. The following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *Lipschitz pushforward preserves integral currents, commutes with boundary, and satisfies the Lipschitz mass bound. Restrictions by bounded Borel coefficients have the usual mass-measure bound; for a Borel set $E\\subset Y$, $$\\|C\\mathbin{\\vrule height 1.4ex depth -0.3ex width .07ex\\vrule height .07ex depth 0ex width .7ex}E\\|=\\|C\\|\\mathbin{\\vrule height 1.4ex depth -0.3ex width .07ex\\vrule height .07ex depth 0ex width .7ex}E.$$ The restricted current need not be normal. If $u:Y\\to\\mathbb R$ is Lipschitz and $C\\in\\mathbf I_k(Y)$, then $C\\mathbin{\\vrule height 1.4ex depth -0.3ex width .07ex\\vrule height .07ex depth 0ex width .7ex}\\{u>t\\}$ is integral for almost every $t$. In particular its boundary is an integral $(k-1)$-cycle.*\n\n2.  *If $C_j\\in\\mathbf I_k(Y)$ and $\\sup_j(\\mathbf M(C_j)+\\mathbf M(\\partial C_j))<\\infty$, a subsequence converges weakly to an integral current. Boundary commutes with weak convergence, and mass is lower semicontinuous.*\n\n\n3.  *Every $C\\in\\mathbf I_k(Y)$ admits a representation by bi-Lipschitz maps $\\phi_i:E_i\\to Y$, where $E_i\\subset\\mathbb R^k$ are bounded Borel sets and the images $Z_i=\\phi_i(E_i)$ are pairwise disjoint Borel sets, and by signed integer multiplicities $\\theta_i\\in L^1(E_i)$, such that $$\\label{eq:chart-action}\n     C(b,\\pi_1,\\ldots,\\pi_k)\n     =\\sum_i\\int_{E_i}\\theta_i(z)b(\\phi_i(z))\n           \\det D\\bigl((\\pi_1,\\ldots,\\pi_k)\\circ\\phi_i\\bigr)(z)\\,dz.$$ The chart summands have summable masses. Zero multiplicities may be discarded. Scalar functions on a Borel chart domain are differentiated by Lipschitz extension to $\\mathbb R^k$; their derivatives are independent of that extension at almost every density point of the domain.*\n\n\n4.  *For $h>0$, the whole-current product $[0,h]\\times C$, with the Euclidean product metric, is an integral $(k+1)$-current and obeys $$\\label{eq:interval-product-boundary}\n     \\partial([0,h]\\times C)\n     =[h]\\times C-[0]\\times C-[0,h]\\times\\partial C.$$ Writing $b_t(x)=b(t,x)$ and $\\pi_{a,t}(x)=\\pi_a(t,x)$, its action is $$\\label{eq:interval-product-action}\n    \\begin{split}\n     &([0,h]\\times C)(b,\\pi_1,\\ldots,\\pi_{k+1})\\\\\n     &\\quad=\\sum_{a=1}^{k+1}(-1)^{a+1}\\int_0^h\n     C\\bigl(b_t\\,\\partial_t\\pi_a(t,\\cdot),\n              \\pi_{1,t},\\ldots,\\widehat{\\pi_{a,t}},\\ldots,\n              \\pi_{k+1,t}\\bigr)\\,dt.\n    \\end{split}$$ The hat indicates omission. The time derivative is interpreted almost everywhere and as a bounded Borel first coefficient in the current action.*\n\n\n5.  *There are finite constants $K_k,L_k$, independent of the cycle, such that every integral $k$-cycle $B$ in $Y$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:coarse-fillings}\n     V(B)\\le K_k\\mathbf M(B)^{(k+1)/k},\\qquad\n     V(B)\\le L_k\\mathbf M(B).$$ Every such $B$ has a mass-minimizing integral filling in $Y$. If integral $k$-cycles $B_j$ have bounded masses and converge weakly to an integral cycle $B$, then $$\\label{eq:filling-continuity}\n     V(B_j-B)\\mathrel{\\begingroup\n      \\let\\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}%\n      PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\\endgroup}0,\n     \\qquad V(B_j)\\mathrel{\\begingroup\n      \\let\\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}%\n      PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\\endgroup}V(B).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-337.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-current-foundations",
      "inputs": [
        "Compact CAT(0) ambient space",
        "Integral-current representation and calculus",
        "Coarse filling results"
      ],
      "method": "Combine integral-current calculus and compactness with geodesic cone inequalities and weak-to-filling continuity.",
      "output": "Finite attained filling volumes, weak compactness, slicing, and whole-current product identities.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-thm-current-background"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-extremizer",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-current-foundations"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-extremizer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $c>0$. If the functional $$B\\mathrel{\\begingroup\n  \\let\\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\let\\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\n  \\let\\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\\endcsname\n  \\let\\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\let\\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\let\\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27FC> >> BDC}%\n  PPaperOriginallongmapsto\\endcsname\\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\\endgroup}V(B)-c\\mathbf M(B)^{q_0}$$ is positive on some integral $n$-cycle in $Y$, it attains a positive maximum on such cycles. A maximizing cycle $A$, with $m=\\mathbf M(A)>0$, satisfies $$\\label{eq:extremizer}\n c\\bigl(m^{q_0}-\\mathbf M(B)^{q_0}\\bigr)\\le V(A-B)\n \\qquad\\text{for every integral \\(n\\)-cycle }B\\text{ in }Y.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-337.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-positive-extremizer",
      "inputs": [

      "method": "Bound the masses in a positive maximizing sequence, take a weak limit, and use filling continuity and mass lower semicontinuity.",
      "output": "A nonzero maximizing cycle and its comparison inequality.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-extremizer"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-euclidean-chart-metrics",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-euclidean-chart-metrics",
          "exact_quote": "For almost every density point $z\\in E_i$, the seminorm in <a href=\"#eq:two-point-metric-differential\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:two-point-metric-differential\">[eq:two-point-metric-differential]</a> is a Euclidean norm. Thus there is a measurable positive definite matrix $G_i(z)$ with $$\\label{eq:chart-metric}\n N_z(w)^2=w^{\\mathsf T}G_i(z)w,\n \\qquad J_i(z)=\\sqrt{\\det G_i(z)}.$$ In particular, $$\\label{eq:centered-metric-differential}\n d(\\phi_i(z+w),\\phi_i(z))=|w|_{G_i(z)}+o(|w|)\n \\quad (z+w\\in E_i).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-337.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-euclidean-chart-metrics",
      "inputs": [
        "Bi-Lipschitz rectifiable charts",
        "Two-moving-point metric differentiation",
        "CAT(0) quadrilateral inequality"
      ],
      "method": "Approximate finite domain configurations at density points and derive the parallelogram law from CAT(0) quadrilateral comparison.",
      "output": "Measurable positive definite chart metric matrices.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-euclidean-chart-metrics"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-quadratic-mass",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-current-foundations"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-quadratic-mass",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that a finite-mass $d$-current $C$ has a representation by countably many determinant integrals with Lipschitz maps $\\psi_i:F_i\\to Y$, Borel sets $F_i\\subset\\mathbb R^d$, and real signed weights $\\vartheta_i$. Suppose that, at almost every point carrying these weights, there is a measurable nonnegative quadratic form $P_i(z)$ such that $$\\label{eq:quadratic-distance-majorant}\n d(\\psi_i(z+w),\\psi_i(z))^2\n \\le P_i(z)[w,w]+o(|w|^2),\\qquad z+w\\in F_i.$$ If the measure $$\\label{eq:quadratic-mass-majorant}\n \\eta=\\sum_i(\\psi_i)_\\#\n       \\bigl(|\\vartheta_i|\\sqrt{\\det P_i}\\,dz\\bigr)$$ is finite, then $\\|C\\|\\le\\eta$. No normality of the separate chart currents is required.",
          "source_id": "PM-337.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-quadratic-mass-upper-bound",
      "inputs": [
        "Determinant-integral representation",
        "Measurable nonnegative quadratic distance majorants",
        "Finite majorant measure"
      ],
      "method": "Bound scalar derivative rows by the quadratic form, apply the determinant inequality, and use minimality of the mass measure.",
      "output": "An upper bound for the current mass measure without normality of individual chart pieces.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-quadratic-mass"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-chart-mass",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-euclidean-chart-metrics",
        "step-quadratic-mass-upper-bound"
      ],
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-chart-mass",
          "exact_quote": "For the representation <a href=\"#eq:chart-action\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:chart-action\">[eq:chart-action]</a>, let $G_i,J_i$ be as in <a href=\"#eq:chart-metric\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:chart-metric\">[eq:chart-metric]</a>. Then $$\\label{eq:chart-mass}\n \\|C\\|=\\sum_i(\\phi_i)_\\#\\bigl(|\\theta_i|J_i\\,dz\\bigr).$$ Thus, with $\\rho_i=|\\theta_i|J_i$, integration against the current mass is integration against $\\rho_i\\,dz$ on the disjoint charts.",
          "source_id": "PM-337.2"
        }

Put $$a(t)=\\mu\\{u>t\\},\\qquad\n U_t=A\\mathbin{\\vrule height 1.4ex depth -0.3ex width .07ex\\vrule height .07ex depth 0ex width .7ex}\\{u>t\\},\\qquad P(t)=\\mathbf M(\\partial U_t),$$ where $U_t$ and its boundary are integral for almost every $t$. At almost every level for which $a(t)\\le\\delta$, $$P(t)\\ge(1-\\eta)n\\omega_n^{1/n}a(t)^{(n-1)/n}.\n \\label{eq:small-set}$$",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-countable-mass-tests",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a nonempty compact metric space and let $k\\ge1$. There is a countable collection $\\mathcal T_k(Z)$ of finite lists $$\\bigl((b_1,\\pi^1),\\ldots,(b_N,\\pi^N)\\bigr),$$ where the $b_j$ are Lipschitz, $\\sum_j|b_j|\\le1$ pointwise, and each $\\pi^j$ is a $k$-tuple of $1$-Lipschitz functions, such that every $k$-current $C$ of finite mass on $Z$ satisfies $$\\mathbf M(C)=\\sup_{\\mathcal T\\in\\mathcal T_k(Z)}\n                  \\sum_{(b,\\pi)\\in\\mathcal T}C(b,\\pi).\n \\label{eq:countable-mass-tests}$$ The collection is independent of $C$.",

      "output": "A current-independent countable family determining total mass.",

          "exact_quote": "For a real Lipschitz function $u$ on $Y$, put $$a(t)=\\mu\\{u>t\\},\\qquad\n e(t)=\\int_{\\{u>t\\}}|Du|^2\\,d\\mu,\n \\qquad P(t)=\\mathbf M\\bigl(\\partial(A\\mathbin{\\vrule height 1.4ex depth -0.3ex width .07ex\\vrule height .07ex depth 0ex width .7ex}\\{u>t\\})\\bigr)$$ at the almost every level where the restriction is integral. Then $$P(t)^2\\le(-a'(t))(-e'(t)),\\qquad\n P(t)\\le\\operatorname{Lip}(u)(-a'(t))\n \\quad\\text{for almost every }t.\n \\label{eq:intrinsic-coarea}$$ The derivatives are the densities of the absolutely continuous parts of the corresponding distribution measures; neither distribution function is assumed absolutely continuous.",
          "source_id": "PM-337.2"

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-sobolev-space",
          "exact_quote": "Identify real Lipschitz functions on $Y$ that agree $\\mu$-almost everywhere. Their completion in the norm $$\\|u\\|_{\\mathcal H}^2=\\|u\\|_2^2+E(u)$$ is a Hilbert space $\\mathcal H$ of functions in $L^2(\\mu)$. Its gradient is a well-defined closed operator with values in the Hilbert space of square-integrable chart covectors. The inclusion $\\mathcal H\\mathrel{\\begingroup\n  \\let\\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\let\\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\n  \\let\\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\\endcsname\n  \\let\\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\let\\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\let\\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}%\n  PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\\endgroup}L^p(\\mu)$ is continuous, and <a href=\"#eq:almost-sobolev\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:almost-sobolev\">[eq:almost-sobolev]</a> holds for all $u\\in\\mathcal H$.*\n\n*The following calculus properties hold.*\n\n1.  *If $u_1,\\ldots,u_k\\in\\mathcal H$ and $\\Phi\\in C^1(\\mathbb R^k)$ has bounded first derivatives, then $\\Phi(u_1,\\ldots,u_k)\\in\\mathcal H$ and $$D\\Phi(u_1,\\ldots,u_k)\n     =\\sum_{a=1}^k\\partial_a\\Phi(u_1,\\ldots,u_k)Du_a.$$*\n\n2.  *For every $u\\in\\mathcal H$ and every fixed $t\\in\\mathbb R$, $Du=0$ almost everywhere on $\\{u=t\\}$. Consequently, the scalar chain rule also holds for Lipschitz, piecewise $C^1$ functions with finitely many corners, with any derivative values assigned at the corners.*\n\n3.  *The map $u\\mathrel{\\begingroup\n      \\let\\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longmapstoP PaperOriginallongmapsto\\endcsname\n      \\let\\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\let\\LongleftrightarrowP PaperOriginalLongleftrightarrow\\endcsname\n      \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF21A6> >> BDC}%\n      PPaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\\endgroup}u_+$ is continuous from $\\mathcal H$ to itself. Every nonnegative element of $\\mathcal H$ has nonnegative Lipschitz approximants converging in $\\mathcal H$.*\n\n4.  *For each fixed center $y\\in Y$, the radial inequality <a href=\"#eq:radial\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:radial\">[eq:radial]</a> holds for every nonnegative $g\\in\\mathcal H$, with $r=d(y,\\cdot)$ and its chart gradient $Dr$.",
          "source_id": "PM-337.2"
        }

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-sharp-integral-filling**: Every compactly supported integral cycle in the stated dimension and ambient geometry admits a compactly supported integral filling with the optimal Euclidean mass coefficient, without a restriction on ambient dimension or integer multiplicities.
- **inv-exact-chart-mass**: The current mass measure equals the sum of the disjoint chart pushforwards weighted by absolute integer multiplicity and the Euclidean chart Jacobian.
- **inv-subthreshold-chord-upper**: The conformally weighted chord second moment is strictly smaller than the opposite averaged chord threshold at almost every center for the normalized hypothetical extremizer.
- **inv-opposite-averaged-chord-bound**: The same chord moments have probability-weighted average at least two; this follows from an all-center moment estimate and CAT(0) variance.
- **inv-domain-perimeter-comparison**: Bounded smooth domains in the stated Cartan–Hadamard manifolds satisfy the Euclidean volume–perimeter inequality, as do relatively compact sets of finite perimeter using ambient perimeter.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-efficient-filling-solver**: Treat the sharp filling theorem as an executable algorithm for constructing a mass-minimizing filling. -> The existence argument passes to an integral weak limit of a minimizing sequence. It supplies neither a finite representation of that limit nor a computational stopping criterion.
- **anti-parametric-mesh-guarantee**: Infer a prescribed topological filling or parametrized surface reconstruction from the current mass bound. -> The filling conclusion controls boundary and mass but does not prescribe topology or parametrization.
- **anti-loop-dimension-transfer**: Apply the main theorem directly to one-dimensional loops. -> The cycle dimension is outside the stated range of the sharp integral filling theorem.
- **anti-arbitrary-ambient-metric**: Transfer the universal Euclidean coefficient to arbitrary geodesic spaces without proving CAT(0) comparison. -> The proof uses CAT(0) comparison to establish Euclidean current-chart norms and to obtain the final variance inequality.
- **anti-chart-restriction-normality**: Assign integral boundaries to arbitrary Borel chart restrictions and perform integration by parts chart by chart. -> A Borel restriction need not be normal. The proof performs the boundary identities on whole currents and localizes resulting measures afterward.
- **anti-fractional-power-chain-rule**: Assume Sobolev regularity of the fractional power of the minimizer when differentiating the weighted chord. -> The fractional power may fail to be continuously differentiable at zero. The proof instead establishes admissibility of the actual composite tests and defines the weighted covector explicitly.
- **anti-automatic-top-dimensional-uniqueness**: Identify an arbitrary integral filling with the integration current of a domain in every ambient space. -> The uniqueness reduction uses the vanishing of compactly supported top-dimensional cycles on a connected, noncompact, oriented Riemannian manifold.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The principal filling result is an existence theorem over integral currents in arbitrary proper CAT(0) spaces, rather than a finite constructive result. The attainment proof states: A minimizing sequence has bounded mass and fixed boundary, so (ii) gives an integral weak limit with that boundary. Lower semicontinuity proves attainment. The sharp estimate is then proved by induction and contradiction through variational extremizers, a Sobolev minimizer and opposing chord bounds. A finite computational input representation, construction procedure and termination guarantee are ABSENT. Finite checks of determinant identities or scalar examples would not decide the quantified filling theorem.

In particular, with $D=\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}Y$, $$L_y\\le\\frac{\\kappa^2D^2}{W}\n             \\int_Y v^{p+2\\beta}\\,d\\mu<\\infty.$$ We study the chord distribution through the transform $$\\label{chord:transform-definition}\n J_y(a)=\\int_Y(1+as^2)^{-n}\\,d\\nu\\qquad(a\\ge0).$$ Our first comparison will give $J_y(a)\\le(1+2aL_y)^{-n/2}$ for every center with $v(y)>0$. Euclidean tangent density will then give a lower bound for $\\liminf_{a\\to\\infty}a^{n/2}J_y(a)$ at almost every center, forcing $$L_y\\le2(W/s_n)^{2/n}<2\n \\quad\\text{for $\\nu$-almost every center $y$}.$$ Section <a href=\"#sec:conclusion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:conclusion\">7</a> will show that the average of these same moments is at least $2$.",

Thus there is a measurable positive definite matrix $G_i(z)$ with $$\\label{eq:chart-metric}\n N_z(w)^2=w^{\\mathsf T}G_i(z)w,\n \\qquad J_i(z)=\\sqrt{\\det G_i(z)}.$$ In particular, $$\\label{eq:centered-metric-differential}\n d(\\phi_i(z+w),\\phi_i(z))=|w|_{G_i(z)}+o(|w|)\n \\quad (z+w\\in E_i).$$",

            "exact_quote": "We have proved the all-center estimate $W^2\\le B_*I(y)$. We recall its proof for the finite measure $v^{p+2\\beta}\\mu$. Compactness of $Y$ and continuity of $I$ provide a minimizer $o\\in Y$. This is a barycenter of the finite measure $v^{p+2\\beta}\\mu$. For $y\\in Y$, let $o_t$ be the point at fraction $t\\in(0,1)$ on the segment from $o$ to $y$. The CAT(0) squared-distance inequality, integrated against $v(x)^{p+2\\beta}\\,d\\mu(x)$, gives $$I(o)\\le I(o_t)\n \\le(1-t)I(o)+tI(y)-t(1-t)B_*d(o,y)^2.$$ Subtracting $(1-t)I(o)$, dividing by $t$, and letting $t\\downarrow0$ proves the variance inequality $$\\label{finish:variance}\n I(y)\\ge I(o)+B_*d(o,y)^2\\qquad(y\\in Y).$$ This uses only the segment from $o$ to $y$.",
