<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The filling theorem can motivate formal specification of geometric existence and mass bounds, but it supplies no executable representation of arbitrary metric currents or procedure for returning the guaranteed filling. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | An integral-current filling does not specify the topology or parametrization needed for a simulated surface, collision geometry, or renderable mesh. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The compact reduction describes a radial retraction in an abstract metric space. It does not provide a finite input representation or an evaluation interface for geodesics and current pushforwards from which a geometric utility could be implemented. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The positive extremizer is obtained through weak compactness, filling continuity, and lower semicontinuity. These arguments establish attainment without a finite search procedure, stopping rule, or runtime bound. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The current representation uses countably many Borel charts and integrable signed integer multiplicities. It supplies an analytical representation, without a finite storage format or certified truncation error for a stored approximation. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The quadratic mass majorant controls a current through measurable infinitesimal forms and determinant integrals. The paper does not turn this analytical estimate into a finite kernel, arithmetic workload, or accelerator execution contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The lower-density result concerns mass at almost every point of an integral current. It supplies no adversarial model, computational hardness assumption, or cryptographic construction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The chord probability measure is derived from a variational minimizer on an extremizing current. It is not a training distribution, and the chord-transform estimate supplies no learning rule or statistical generalization guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves attainment of an infinite-dimensional critical variational problem and excludes loss of critical mass. This can inform analytical reasoning about minimization, but it does not specify a numerical optimizer, discretization, or computable convergence certificate. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The averaged chord bound uses a continuous weighted measure and CAT(0) distance variance. It gives no communication protocol, network cut bound, or agreement procedure for distributed participants. |
| other (Geometric measure theory and metric geometry) | False | none | not_applicable | stated | The central contribution is an analytical existence theorem for sharp integral fillings in proper CAT(0) spaces, with a domain consequence in Cartan–Hadamard manifolds. Its mathematical relevance is direct, but a finite constructive engineering realization is ABSENT. |

Then $$\\label{eq:chart-mass}\n \\|C\\|=\\sum_i(\\phi_i)_\\#\\bigl(|\\theta_i|J_i\\,dz\\bigr).$$ Thus, with $\\rho_i=|\\theta_i|J_i$, integration against the current mass is integration against $\\rho_i\\,dz$ on the disjoint charts.",

          "exact_quote": "Suppose that a finite-mass $d$-current $C$ has a representation by countably many determinant integrals with Lipschitz maps $\\psi_i:F_i\\to Y$, Borel sets $F_i\\subset\\mathbb R^d$, and real signed weights $\\vartheta_i$. Suppose that, at almost every point carrying these weights, there is a measurable nonnegative quadratic form $P_i(z)$ such that $$\\label{eq:quadratic-distance-majorant}\n d(\\psi_i(z+w),\\psi_i(z))^2\n \\le P_i(z)[w,w]+o(|w|^2),\\qquad z+w\\in F_i.$$ If the measure $$\\label{eq:quadratic-mass-majorant}\n \\eta=\\sum_i(\\psi_i)_\\#\n       \\bigl(|\\vartheta_i|\\sqrt{\\det P_i}\\,dz\\bigr)$$ is finite, then $\\|C\\|\\le\\eta$.       "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-chord-transform",
          "exact_quote": "For every center $y$ with $v(y)>0$, and every $a\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:chord-transform}\n J_y(a)=\\int_Y(1+as^2)^{-n}\\,d\\nu\n                  \\le(1+2aL_y)^{-n/2},$$ where $s$ and $L_y$ are defined in <a href=\"#chord:data\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"chord:data\">[chord:data]</a>.",
          "source_object": "Distribution comparison proposition"
        },

          "anchor": "chord:normalization",

By Lemma <a href=\"#lem:powers\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:powers\">21</a>, $v$ has all finite positive moments and $$\\label{chord:weighted-energy}\n \\int_Y v^q\\lvert Dv\\rvert^2\\,d\\mu<\\infty\\qquad(q\\ge0).$$ Choose a finite nonnegative Borel representative of $v$; changing it on a $\\mu$-null set has no effect on these assertions.",

        }

There exists a nonzero nonnegative $v\\in\\mathcal H$ minimizing $Q(u)/\\|u\\|_p^2$ over nonzero $u\\in\\mathcal H$ and satisfying $$\\begin{split}\n 0<W:=\\int v^p\\,d\\mu\n       =\\left(\\frac\\Lambda n\\right)^{n/2}<s_n,\n \\qquad Q(v)=nW.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:minimizer-normalization}$$ For every $\\psi\\in\\mathcal H$ it satisfies the weak equation $$4\\beta\\int Dv\\cdot D\\psi\\,d\\mu+n\\int v\\psi\\,d\\mu\n   =n\\int v^{p-1}\\psi\\,d\\mu.\n \\label{eq:euler}$$",

      "applies": false,

      "domain": "distributed_systems",
      "implementation_checks": [],
      "maturity": "not_applicable",
      "proposed_domain": null,
      "pseudocode": null,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-opposite-average",
          "exact_quote": "Define $$\\label{finish:weighted-moment}\n B_*:=\\int_Y v^{p+2\\beta}\\,d\\mu,\n \\qquad\n I(y):=\\int_Y d(y,x)^2v(x)^{p+2\\beta}\\,d\\mu(x).$$ Then $0<B_*<\\infty$, the function $I$ is finite and continuous on $Y$, and $$\\label{finish:all-centers}\n W^2\\le B_*I(y)\\qquad\\text{for every }y\\in Y.$$ In particular, for the chord moments $$L_y=\\int_Y\n       \\bigl[v(y)^\\beta d(y,x)v(x)^\\beta\\bigr]^2\\,d\\nu(x),$$ one has $$\\label{finish:opposite-average}\n \\int_Y L_y\\,d\\nu(y)\\ge2.$$",

The coefficient is optimal.",

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-proper-cat0-ambient** (hypothesis): The universal sharp filling bound requires a proper CAT(0) ambient space. An extension to arbitrary geodesic spaces is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Invoke the universal Euclidean filling coefficient without establishing properness and CAT(0) comparison.']
- **limit-integral-compact-cycle-input** (hypothesis): The input must be an integral, compactly supported cycle of the stated integer dimension. Arbitrary real multiplicities, nonzero boundary, and unrestricted noncompact support are outside the theorem's guarantee. Blocked use: ['Apply the filling conclusion to a current with nonzero boundary.', 'Replace integer multiplicities by arbitrary real weights without a separate theorem.', 'Drop compact support from the input hypothesis.']
- **limit-loop-dimension** (formal_scope): The main filling theorem starts at cycle dimension two. Its statement does not supply the loop-filling result discussed separately in the predecessor section. Blocked use: ['Apply the main theorem directly to one-dimensional loops.']
- **limit-unprescribed-filling-topology** (formal_scope): The filling controls current boundary and mass without prescribing topology or parametrization. Blocked use: ['Infer a topological disc, embedded surface, or prescribed parametrized extension from the mass estimate.']
- **limit-variational-filling-attainment** (nonconstructive): Least filling mass is attained through weak compactness and lower semicontinuity. A finite representation of the minimizing filling, terminating construction, and computational complexity are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Advertise the existence proof as an executable minimal-filling solver.', 'Claim a numerical convergence rate from variational attainment alone.']
- **limit-positive-extremizer-premise** (hypothesis): The extremizer lemma requires positivity of the penalized filling functional on an integral cycle. The normalized extremizing cycle belongs to the hypothetical violation argument. Blocked use: ['Assert existence of a nonzero positive extremizer when the positivity premise has not been established.', 'Treat the normalized contradiction cycle as an available input object for arbitrary cycles.']
- **limit-chart-quadratic-majorant** (hypothesis): The quadratic mass estimate requires a determinant-integral representation, measurable nonnegative distance majorants at almost every weighted point, and finiteness of their induced measure. Blocked use: ['Infer current mass control from a determinant formula without proving the distance majorant.', 'Apply the estimate when the induced majorant measure is not finite.']
- **limit-borel-restriction-boundaries** (formal_scope): A Borel restriction need not be normal. Whole-current boundary identities cannot automatically be applied independently to every chart restriction. Blocked use: ['Assign finite boundary mass to arbitrary Borel chart pieces.', 'Perform chartwise integration by parts using an unproved normality premise.']
- **limit-density-almost-everywhere** (formal_scope): The Euclidean lower density is a lower-limit estimate at almost every point for the current mass measure, using open balls. It is not an all-center estimate or a uniform finite-radius bound. Blocked use: ['Assert the density bound at every selected center.', 'Turn the lower-limit density statement into a certified finite-radius error estimate.']
- **limit-radial-extremizer-regime** (hypothesis): The radial first variation is proved for the normalized hypothetical extremizing cycle and nonnegative admissible tests. It is not asserted for every integral cycle. Blocked use: ['Apply the normalized radial inequality to an arbitrary cycle.', 'Insert signed tests without an additional argument.']
- **limit-small-set-induction-and-threshold** (hypothesis): The small-set perimeter argument assumes the sharp filling theorem in the lower cycle dimension and applies only at almost every integral restriction level within its small-mass threshold. Blocked use: ['Use the perimeter estimate for unrestricted superlevel mass.', 'Omit the lower-dimensional filling input.', 'Require the estimate at exceptional slicing levels.']
- **limit-countable-mass-supremum** (nonconstructive): The mass-test lemma recovers mass through a supremum over a countable family of finite lists. It does not furnish a finite exhaustive mass test or an effective truncation error. Blocked use: ['Treat a fixed finite subset of the test family as deciding total current mass.', 'Claim a computable stopping rule for the countable supremum.']
- **limit-coarea-distribution-densities** (formal_scope): Intrinsic coarea uses densities of the absolutely continuous parts of distribution measures. The distribution functions may also have singular parts. Blocked use: ['Assume that the distribution functions are absolutely continuous.', 'Identify the integral of their ordinary derivatives with the full distributional variation without accounting for singular parts.']
- **limit-rearrangement-small-support** (hypothesis): The energy-controlled rearrangement requires a nonnegative Lipschitz function whose positivity set meets the small-support threshold. The generalized inverse accommodates atoms and singular continuous distributions. Blocked use: ['Apply the small-support energy estimate directly to an arbitrary signed function or unrestricted support.', 'Replace the generalized inverse argument by an unproved smooth-distribution assumption.']
- **limit-radial-sobolev-class** (hypothesis): The stated sharp Euclidean Sobolev lemma concerns nonnegative radial nonincreasing compactly supported Lipschitz functions in the higher-dimensional regime. Blocked use: ['Use this lemma alone as the proof of an unrestricted nonradial Sobolev theorem.', 'Apply its critical-exponent argument in the base cycle dimension.']
- **limit-global-sobolev-lower-order-term** (approximation): The global critical estimate includes an arbitrarily small loss in the sharp coefficient and a lower-order term. Its finite constant is independent of the tested function but is not eliminated by the proposition. Blocked use: ['Delete the lower-order term from the global estimate.', 'Replace the almost sharp coefficient by an exact global coefficient without further proof.']
- **limit-completed-space-compactness** (nonconstructive): The closed chart gradient and compact lower-norm embedding concern the Sobolev completion on the extremizing cycle's mass measure. Compact inclusion supplies subsequential convergence rather than an effective approximation rate. Blocked use: ['Transfer the compact embedding to an arbitrary metric-measure space.', 'Infer a computable convergence rate for a numerical minimizing sequence.']
- **limit-subthreshold-minimizer** (hypothesis): The critical minimizer is obtained in the normalized contradiction regime below the sphere-area threshold. Its existence and moment estimates do not assert smoothness, strict positivity everywhere, or essential boundedness. Blocked use: ['Assume the same minimizer-existence argument above the critical threshold.', 'Use smoothness, everywhere positivity, or essential boundedness as an unstated premise for nonlinear tests.']
- **limit-polynomial-composite-tests** (hypothesis): The composite chain rule requires boundedness of the distance-type argument and its gradient, continuous differentiability of the composite on a neighborhood of the relevant strip, and polynomial growth of its value and first derivatives. Blocked use: ['Apply the composite chain rule to arbitrary nonsmooth or uncontrolled-growth functions.', 'Replace the proved composite tests by an unproved chain rule for the isolated fractional minimizer power.']
- **limit-chord-center-and-measure** (formal_scope): The transform comparison fixes a center with positive minimizer value and a nonnegative parameter. The tangent-density and strict moment bounds hold almost everywhere for the minimizer-derived probability measure. Blocked use: ['Apply the transform estimate to an unrelated sampled probability distribution.', 'Promote the almost-everywhere strict moment bound to every center.', 'Assume Sobolev membership of the weighted chord itself.']
- **limit-variance-and-averaging-regime** (hypothesis): The opposite averaged chord estimate uses the compact CAT(0) space, the normalized minimizer, its finite weighted moments, and a barycenter variance argument. It is not a general bound for arbitrary distance kernels. Blocked use: ['Transfer the averaged bound to an arbitrary metric space or arbitrary endpoint weights.', 'Infer discrete network or consensus guarantees from the continuous chord average.']
- **limit-domain-corollary-scope** (hypothesis): The domain corollary requires the stated Cartan–Hadamard manifold dimension and bounded smooth domains, or relatively compact sets of finite ambient perimeter. Blocked use: ['Apply the domain corollary to arbitrary curvature regimes.', 'Drop boundedness or relative compactness.', 'Substitute a different perimeter convention without proving agreement.']
- **limit-top-dimensional-uniqueness** (hypothesis): The filling-identification argument requires a compactly supported top-dimensional cycle in a connected, noncompact, oriented Riemannian manifold. Blocked use: ['Infer uniqueness of all fillings in arbitrary codimension.', 'Apply top-dimensional vanishing on a compact manifold.', 'Identify a filling with a domain integration current without checking the difference current and the manifold hypotheses.']
- **limit-sharpness-without-general-rigidity** (formal_scope): Euclidean balls demonstrate optimality of the universal filling coefficient. A classification of all equality cases in arbitrary proper CAT(0) spaces is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Infer general equality rigidity or uniqueness of extremal geometry from coefficient optimality.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 337 (`lean/docs/337.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Sharp integral fillings in $\mathrm{CAT}(0)$ spaces](../../preprints/Sharp-integral-fillings-in-CAT(0)-spaces-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves the sharp Euclidean filling inequality for every compactly supported integral $n$-cycle in a proper $\mathrm{CAT}(0)$ space, for $n\ge2$. It constructs a compactly supported integral filling whose mass is at most $C_n$ times the boundary mass to the power $(n+1)/n$, where $C_n$ is the Euclidean isoperimetric coefficient. Integer multiplicities and the ambient dimension are unrestricted.
>
> A second statement proves that this coefficient is optimal by giving, in Euclidean space, a cycle for which every compactly supported integral filling has at least that mass. This yields the selected integral-current form of the Cartan–Hadamard isoperimetric conjecture.

Comparator source `SharpCAT0Filling.lean` (5830 bytes, sha256 `e08a884c0c24078e6b83fc1fa758e206d3603cfe348eda0b689ec322da2a0a74`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

open Set Filter MeasureTheory
open scoped Topology ENNReal NNReal

namespace CAT0Fillings

attribute [local instance] Classical.propDecidable

universe u

abbrev Euc (k : ℕ) := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin k)
abbrev Functional (X : Type u) (k : ℕ) := (X → ℝ) → (Fin k → X → ℝ) → ℝ

section MetricCurrents

variable {X : Type u} [MetricSpace X] [MeasurableSpace X] [BorelSpace X]

def BoundedLip (b : X → ℝ) : Prop :=
  (∃ K : ℝ≥0, LipschitzWith K b) ∧ ∃ M : ℝ, ∀ x, |b x| ≤ M

def Admissible {k : ℕ} (b : X → ℝ) (π : Fin k → X → ℝ) : Prop :=
  BoundedLip b ∧ ∀ i, ∃ K : ℝ≥0, LipschitzWith K (π i)

def Controls {k : ℕ} (T : Functional X k) (μ : Measure X) : Prop :=
  ∀ (b : X → ℝ) (π : Fin k → X → ℝ), BoundedLip b →
    (∀ i, LipschitzWith 1 (π i)) →
    |T b π| ≤ ∫ x, |b x| ∂μ

noncomputable def mass {k : ℕ} (T : Functional X k) : ℝ :=
  sInf {r : ℝ | ∃ μ : Measure X,
    IsFiniteMeasure μ ∧ Controls T μ ∧ r = μ.real univ}

structure IsMetricCurrent {k : ℕ} (T : Functional X k) : Prop where
  offDomain : ∀ b π, ¬Admissible b π → T b π = 0
  linearFirst : ∀ b₁ b₂ π (a c : ℝ), BoundedLip b₁ → BoundedLip b₂ →
    (∀ i, ∃ K : ℝ≥0, LipschitzWith K (π i)) →
    T (fun x => a * b₁ x + c * b₂ x) π = a * T b₁ π + c * T b₂ π
  linearCoord : ∀ b π i f (a c : ℝ), Admissible b π →
    (∃ K : ℝ≥0, LipschitzWith K f) →
    T b (Function.update π i (fun x => a * π i x + c * f x)) =
      a * T b π + c * T b (Function.update π i f)
  sequentialContinuity : ∀ b π (πs : ℕ → Fin k → X → ℝ), BoundedLip b →
    (∀ i, ∃ K : ℝ≥0, ∀ j, LipschitzWith K (πs j i)) →
    (∀ i x, Tendsto (fun j => πs j i x) atTop (𝓝 (π i x))) →
    Tendsto (fun j => T b (πs j)) atTop (𝓝 (T b π))
  locality : ∀ b π, Admissible b π →
    (∃ (i : Fin k) (U : Set X) (c : ℝ), IsOpen U ∧ Function.support b ⊆ U ∧
      ∀ x ∈ U, π i x = c) → T b π = 0
  finiteMass : ∃ μ : Measure X, IsFiniteMeasure μ ∧ Controls T μ

noncomputable def boundarySucc {k : ℕ} (T : Functional X (k + 1)) : Functional X k :=
  fun b π => if Admissible b π then T (fun _ => 1) (Matrix.vecCons b π) else 0

noncomputable def boundary : {k : ℕ} → Functional X k → Functional X (k - 1)
  | 0, _ => fun _ _ => 0
  | _ + 1, T => boundarySucc T

structure IntegerChart (X : Type u) [MetricSpace X] (k : ℕ) where
  domain : Set (Euc k)
  borel : MeasurableSet domain
  bounded : Bornology.IsBounded domain
  param : domain → X
  bilipschitz : ∃ L U : ℝ≥0, LipschitzWith L param ∧ AntilipschitzWith U param
  multiplicity : Euc k → ℤ
  integrable : Integrable (fun z => (multiplicity z : ℝ)) (volume.restrict domain)

namespace IntegerChart

variable {k : ℕ} (C : IntegerChart X k)

noncomputable def scalar (f : X → ℝ) (z : Euc k) : ℝ :=
  if hz : z ∈ C.domain then f (C.param ⟨z, hz⟩) else 0

noncomputable def jacobian (π : Fin k → X → ℝ) (z : Euc k) : ℝ :=
  Matrix.det (fun i j =>
    (fderivWithin ℝ (C.scalar (π i)) C.domain z) (EuclideanSpace.single j 1))

noncomputable def action : Functional X k :=
  fun b π => if Admissible b π then
    ∫ z in C.domain, (C.multiplicity z : ℝ) * C.scalar b z * C.jacobian π z
  else 0

def image : Set X := Set.range C.param

end IntegerChart

def IntegerRectifiable {k : ℕ} (T : Functional X k) : Prop :=
  ∃ C : ℕ → IntegerChart X k,
    Pairwise (fun i j => Disjoint (C i).image (C j).image) ∧
    (∀ i, IsMetricCurrent (C i).action) ∧
    Summable (fun i => mass (C i).action) ∧
    ∀ b π, T b π = ∑' i, (C i).action b π

def IsIntegral : (k : ℕ) → Functional X k → Prop
  | 0, T => IsMetricCurrent T ∧ IntegerRectifiable T
  | _ + 1, T => IsMetricCurrent T ∧ IntegerRectifiable T ∧
      IsMetricCurrent (boundarySucc T) ∧ IntegerRectifiable (boundarySucc T)

abbrev IntegralCurrent (X : Type u) [MetricSpace X] [MeasurableSpace X] [BorelSpace X]
    (k : ℕ) := { T : Functional X k // IsIntegral k T }

def CompactlySupported {k : ℕ} (T : Functional X k) : Prop :=
  ∃ K : Set X, IsCompact K ∧
    ∀ b π, Admissible b π → (∀ x ∈ K, b x = 0) → T b π = 0

def IsCycle {k : ℕ} (T : Functional X k) : Prop := boundary T = fun _ _ => 0

end MetricCurrents

section Geometry
variable (X : Type u) [MetricSpace X]

def IsCAT0 : Prop :=
  ∃ segment : X → X → ℝ → X,
    (∀ x y, segment x y 0 = x ∧ segment x y 1 = y) ∧
    (∀ x y s t, s ∈ Icc (0 : ℝ) 1 → t ∈ Icc (0 : ℝ) 1 →
      dist (segment x y s) (segment x y t) = |s - t| * dist x y) ∧
    (∀ o x y s t, s ∈ Icc (0 : ℝ) 1 → t ∈ Icc (0 : ℝ) 1 →
      dist (segment o x s) (segment o y t) ^ 2 ≤
        (s * dist o x - t * dist o y) ^ 2 +
          s * t * ((dist x y) ^ 2 - (dist o x - dist o y) ^ 2))

end Geometry

noncomputable def omega (k : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  (volume (Metric.ball (0 : Euc k) 1)).toReal
noncomputable def sphereArea (n : ℕ) : ℝ := (n + 1 : ℝ) * omega (n + 1)
noncomputable def fillingCoefficient (n : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  1 / ((n + 1 : ℝ) * (sphereArea n) ^ (1 / (n : ℝ)))
noncomputable def fillingPower (n : ℕ) : ℝ := (n + 1 : ℝ) / (n : ℝ)

theorem sharp_integral_filling
    (X : Type u) [MetricSpace X] [MeasurableSpace X] [BorelSpace X] [ProperSpace X]
    (hX : IsCAT0 X) (n : ℕ) (hn : 2 ≤ n) (T : IntegralCurrent X n)
    (hTc : CompactlySupported T.val) (hTz : IsCycle T.val) :
    ∃ S : IntegralCurrent X (n + 1), CompactlySupported S.val ∧
      boundarySucc S.val = T.val ∧
      mass S.val ≤ fillingCoefficient n * (mass T.val) ^ fillingPower n := by
  sorry

end CAT0Fillings

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Every compactly supported integral <i>n</i>-cycle, <i>n</i> ≥ 2, in a proper CAT(0) space bounds a compactly supported integral current with the sharp Euclidean mass bound. The theorem allows arbitrary integer multiplicities and unrestricted ambient dimension. In particular, we prove the Euclidean Cartan–Hadamard isoperimetric conjecture in dimensions at least three.
